# Gate 2 — collated findings (iteration 1)

Reviewer A: gpt-5.6-sol @ xhigh (codex exec, read-only) — BLOCKED, 16 findings.
Reviewer B: claude-fable-5 (fresh-context subagent) — BLOCKED, 26 findings, with live reproductions.
Full verdicts: reviewer-a/iteration-01/verdict.md, reviewer-b/iteration-01/verdict.md.

Deduped work list, dependency-ordered. [A#/B#] = source finding numbers.

## Blocking

1. **Hook script never runs in production** [B1] — hook.mjs has no main entry (`runHook` exported, never invoked as a script) and forwards stdin via an `{input}` option async execFile doesn't support. The shipped Claude Code adapter checkpoints exactly never. Fix: argv main guard building real io; stdin via spawn/execFileSync input; spawn-level integration test executing the script exactly as hooks.json does.
2. **Session ownership seams** [A2, B3, B5] — (a) auto-seed never adopts the incoming stable hint → foreign-session isolation never engages; (b) receive defaults sessionHint `cli:<host>` marked stable, adapters pass no --session → first real post-receive hook rejected as foreign. Fix: adopt-on-seed from first stable event; defaulted receive hint marked unstable + checkpoint adopts first stable same-platform hint after a receive; contract test with a hint receive never saw.
3. **StopFailure never marks limit-hit** [A3, B14] — detect verdict discarded; handoff.reasonClass stays null; SessionStart pending notice can never fire for unsealed limit death. Fix: merge-reducer event (e.g. `handoff.update`/limit-hit) emitted by the hook on rate_limit; assert persisted bundle state.
4. **Receive commit not atomic** [A1, B11, B12, B2, B13] — token check + three mutations under separate locks; guardedWrite unused in the mutation path; lock takeover notes lack seq/dedupeKey (NaN journalSeq poisoning — B2's live repro lost a seal); processAlive treats EPERM as dead and startTime is never compared. Fix: single withLock across the whole commit with lock-context store variants; token additionally binds handoff.status + receive_log.length; fence-check before each write; lock notes through seq/dedupeKey allocation; applyEvent hardened against keyless events; EPERM=alive + real start-time compare.
5. **No root discovery / --root ignored** [A5, B10] — all commands use io.cwd verbatim; subdirectory runs seed stray bundles. Fix: central resolver (.handoff|baton.config.json ancestor → git toplevel → cwd; stop at nested .git; --root override) threaded through every command.
6. **Threat model gaps** [A6, A7, A8, B6, B7] — no symlink refusal (purge follows links outside the repo); regex lint misses `(a|a)*x`-class patterns and no runtime match guard (93.8 s hang reproduced); nested bundle shapes unvalidated (string constraints crash render; absolute/`..` paths stored verbatim). Fix: lstat + realpath jail on the managed tree; bounded match guard (worker or load-time pathological probe); deep schema validation + path normalization/jailing.
7. **Adapter command paths break outside the repo** [A10, B17] — commands say `node core/bin/baton.mjs`; only resolves inside baton itself. Fix: `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/core/bin/baton.mjs` in all three command bodies.
8. **Codex/Cursor SessionStart unguarded** [A11, B15] — unconditional full receive prep: exit-1 noise with no bundle, resume prompt for own open bundle, wrong output shape for Cursor. Fix: pending check (sealed-or-limit-hit AND foreign) via a cheap status path; harness-specific context output; always exit 0.
9. **Templates below contract** [A13, B18] — AGENTS.md.tpl missing most mandated sections incl. the attribution invariant; no init --check drift test. Fix: render the ten-section contract within budgets; add init --check.
10. **Unwired plan features in checkpoint pipeline** [A4, B8] — no git capture on checkpoint, no opt-in transcript capture at PreCompact (config key ships dead), compact() never called at runtime. Fix: bounded git refresh on important checkpoints; config-gated redacted tail capture; compaction in the snapshot write path. (Transcript: implement minimal plan-compliant version — capture.transcriptTail respected, redaction patterns, bounded.)
11. **recover unimplemented + envelope gaps** [A14, B9] — recover in the frozen list returns not-implemented; checkpoint --json emits no envelope; usage errors bypass the envelope contract. Fix: cmdRecover over recoverLock; centralize envelope emission.
12. **Acceptance-suite fidelity** [A16] — concurrency tests use pre-created locks/sequential commits; StopFailure tests assert argv not persisted state; complicit test patterns B called out (echoed hints, fake execFile input). Fix: barrier-controlled child-process concurrency tests; adapter-to-core state tests; symlink/traversal fixtures. Test changes re-enter test-verifier review.

## Major

13. **Doctor gaps** [A9, B16, B25] — version floors, trust/canary states, network-fs detection, --version capped at installed, probe cache consumed by receive/remap (+ probe snapshot into the token).
14. **Cursor afterFileEdit debounce missing** [A12, B26] — checkpoint --debounce flag; cursor hook uses it.
15. **.bak can be overwritten by a corrupt snapshot** [A15] — validate before backup; journal seed events for lossless rebuild.

## Minor

16. reason-class validation on finalize [B19]; purge marker never auto-resumed [B20]; marker reconciliation writes on read paths un-locked [B21]; status bypasses loadBundle [B22]; hook errors unlogged / no .handoff/log retention [B23]; history freeze copy non-atomic [B24].

## Clean

Git history: sole author, zero AI attribution (verified independently by both reviewers). Attribution invariant suite, lock refusal semantics, recovery ladder, scaffold purity, init idempotency, detect exit codes, prompt caps, token drift binding — all confirmed faithful.

docs/design/core.md:135:## CLI contract
docs/design/core.md:137:Commands: `checkpoint`, `finalize`, `detect`, `remap`, `receive`, `init`, `doctor`, `status`, `purge-transcript`, `recover`. Exit codes: `0` ok · `1` command failure · `2` usage error · `10` usage-limit · `11` throttle · `12` auth · `13` other-error. `--json`: exactly one `{ok, data, warnings, error}` envelope on stdout, diagnostics on stderr. Checkpoint soft-fails to exit 0 in hook contexts (`--strict` opts out). `wrap` is reserved, not implemented in v1.
docs/plans/2026-07-11-baton-v1.md:1:# baton — cross-harness workflow failover (v1 plan)
docs/plans/2026-07-11-baton-v1.md:5:## Context
docs/plans/2026-07-11-baton-v1.md:11:## Locked decisions (from Saksham, this session)
docs/plans/2026-07-11-baton-v1.md:28:## Research findings that shape the design (verified 2026-07-11)
docs/plans/2026-07-11-baton-v1.md:30:- **Codex plugin (installed, v1.0.4) is the implementation pattern**: single Node entrypoint invoked as `node "${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/scripts/<x>.mjs" <subcmd>`; hooks in `hooks/hooks.json` (SessionStart/SessionEnd/Stop) read event JSON on stdin and can emit `{"decision":"block","reason":…}`; commands/agents/skills as markdown with frontmatter; per-workspace state dir; structured-output JSON schemas for reviews. It has **no usage-limit handling** — our failover logic is net-new.
docs/plans/2026-07-11-baton-v1.md:33:  - Claude Code: **`StopFailure` hook event** fires when a turn ends on an API error, with matcher filtering by error type (`rate_limit`, `overloaded`, `billing_error`, `server_error`, …) — the primary, structured limit-death signal. Output/exit code ignored (observability-only), so a checkpoint there is safe by construction. Text fallback: the stable strings `You've hit your session limit · resets …` / `weekly limit` / `Opus limit`.
docs/plans/2026-07-11-baton-v1.md:34:  - Codex (>= 0.144): **lifecycle hooks exist** (`SessionStart`, `PreToolUse`, `PostToolUse`, `PreCompact`, `PostCompact`, `UserPromptSubmit`, `SubagentStop`, `Stop`; configured via `~/.codex/hooks.json`, `<repo>/.codex/hooks.json`, or config.toml `[hooks]`; stdin JSON with `session_id`, `cwd`, `model`, `transcript_path`). No StopFailure equivalent → limit death bounded by last Stop checkpoint. Text fallback: `You've hit your usage limit…`; `codex exec --json` exits non-zero.
docs/plans/2026-07-11-baton-v1.md:40:## Prompting-guide distillation (for the AGENTS.md/CLAUDE.md templates)
docs/plans/2026-07-11-baton-v1.md:46:## Design
docs/plans/2026-07-11-baton-v1.md:50:### Core engine (`core/`)
docs/plans/2026-07-11-baton-v1.md:54:**CLI surface** (`baton <cmd> --json`): `checkpoint`, `finalize`, `detect`, `remap`, `receive`, `init`, `doctor`, `status`, `purge-transcript`, `recover` (forced lock recovery). Frozen exit-code contract: `0` ok · `1` command failure · `2` usage error · `10` usage-limit · `11` throttle · `12` auth · `13` other-error. `--json` mode prints exactly one envelope `{ok, data, warnings, error}` on stdout. `baton wrap -- <cmd>` (PTY supervisor) is **deferred post-v1** — hook-driven detection suffices; the name/contract is reserved.
docs/plans/2026-07-11-baton-v1.md:56:**Bundle** (`.handoff/`): single active bundle = `bundle.json` (atomic tmp+rename snapshot, `.bak` of previous good) + `journal.ndjson` (append-only events, torn-tail tolerant) + rendered `HANDOFF.md` + `history/` (unified retention: last 10 entries **per type** — finalize freezes, takeover archives, receive archives — each = frozen snapshot plus rotated journal; receipts needed for replay/audit are always preserved within retained entries). Load = snapshot + replay journal entries past `journalSeq`; recovery paths: `.bak` + replay, or full journal rebuild. Schema `baton/bundle@1`: origin platform/model, task goal/constraints/acceptance, plan steps with status, capped decisions log, files touched, role assignments, git branch/HEAD/dirty summary, handoff finalize block (`status: open|sealed|received` + `receive_log[]`), compaction markers. Size discipline is **deterministic truncation only** (decisions capped first-20+last-150 with marker; no LLM summarization).
docs/plans/2026-07-11-baton-v1.md:58:**Concurrency** (Gate-1 additions, iterations 1+2): all bundle mutations run under a repo-scoped lock — `.handoff/lock` acquired via atomic `mkdir`/`O_EXCL`, containing `{writerId: <platform>-<pid>-<sessionHint>, fencingToken: <random per acquisition>, acquiredAt, heartbeatAt}`. **Lock model (iterations 3+4)**: mutation locks are **operation-scoped** — acquired for the duration of one command's mutation and released, never held across turns; logical session ownership lives in `bundle.json` (the active `origin`/generation), not in the lock. Lock metadata (host, pid, process start time, fencing token) is written atomically with acquisition; a crash between lock creation and metadata publication reads as torn metadata. Recovery rules, conservative on every OS: automatic recovery only when the owner is **provably dead** (same host, pid gone or pid/start-time mismatch); a same-host owner confirmed alive is **refused** — including `recover --force` — with the instruction to terminate that process first; missing/torn/cross-host/unverifiable owner metadata is treated as **unsupported for automatic recovery** (refusal with explanation), never as safe-to-steal. Fencing tokens remain a fast-abort layer. Tests: force attempted against a live paused writer (refused), crash between lock creation and metadata publication, owner paused after its final token check, torn-metadata refusal. Journal entries carry `writerId`; sequence numbers are allocated under the lock. **Semantic isolation**: v1 enforces one active task — a checkpoint whose `sessionHint`/origin doesn't match the active bundle is rejected with an actionable warning (`--take-over` supersedes: archives the current bundle to `history/` and starts fresh), so two unrelated sessions can't merge goals/files/decisions into one handoff. `receive` is a core-owned two-phase transaction. `receive --prepare` validates + resolves roles + returns a one-time receipt token and the resume prompt without mutating state; the token **binds** the bundle revision (journalSeq), normalized intake, config digest, target platform+session, the probe snapshot used for remap, **and a content-sensitive prepare-time git digest** — HEAD + branch + hashes of the staged and unstaged diffs + a bounded untracked-files digest (path + size + content hash, capped) — so a file that stays `M` but changes content still invalidates the token. `--commit` re-derives and compares every bound input, rejecting any drift (intervening checkpoint, config edit, HEAD move, content change, probe change) and requiring re-prepare; each declared drift input has its own rejection test. `receive --commit <token>` performs one atomic transition: `sealed → received` (normal) or `open → received` (the primary failure case: limit death before finalize; commit writes a receive-generated degraded seal recording intake origin/reason, flagged `degraded-seal`) — and in the same transition **adopts the receiving platform/session as the bundle's active owner and opens a fresh writable generation** (`generation` counter incremented, `status: open`), archiving the prior seal + receipt to `history/`. The receiving session's subsequent checkpoints are therefore first-party, and the bundle is immediately receivable again if the new platform also dies — chained A→B→C failover is contract-tested with checkpoints fired immediately after each receive. Cancellation or a competing receiver leaves state untouched; double-commit fails cleanly. Identical semantics on every adapter; six-direction contract tests cover sealed + unsealed variants. Tests: concurrent writers, provably-dead takeover, fencing-token abort incl. pause-after-final-check, foreign-session rejection/take-over, prepare-vs-checkpoint races, prepare-without-commit, double-commit, competing receivers, chained failovers.
docs/plans/2026-07-11-baton-v1.md:60:**Journal rotation & crash recovery** (Gate-1 additions, iterations 1–3): on every finalize, crash-safe order = write a rotation marker → write frozen snapshot to `history/` → rename `journal.ndjson` alongside it → start a fresh journal seeded with the snapshot's `journalSeq` → clear marker. On load, a present marker deterministically rolls the rotation forward (if the snapshot landed) or back (if not); the same marker pattern governs interrupted forced takeovers and interrupted purges (purge resumes from its marker until the whole tree is clean). Retention: last 10 rotations pruned with the history. Doctor warns at journal > 5 MB; perf test: 10k-event replay < 1 s. **Filesystem support boundary: v1 supports local filesystems only** — lock and rename atomicity are not guaranteed on network/shared mounts (NFS/SMB/cloud-sync folders); doctor detects and warns, README states it.
docs/plans/2026-07-11-baton-v1.md:62:**Root discovery & degraded modes** (Gate-1 additions, iterations 1+2): repo root = nearest ancestor containing `.handoff/` or `baton.config.json`, else `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` (worktree-aware: linked worktrees resolve to their own toplevel, shared state stays per-worktree), else cwd — `--root <path>` overrides; nested/monorepo launches therefore resolve to the same bundle deterministically, but **discovery never crosses the boundary of a nested independent git repository** (a `.git` between cwd and a candidate root stops the walk) unless `--root` says otherwise. Non-git directories: supported — `git` section is null, git-dependent features degrade with explicit warnings. Read-only sandboxes (EACCES/EROFS on `.handoff/`): checkpoint fails open with a warning naming the user-executed fallback (`baton checkpoint` run manually outside the sandbox; `receive --print-prompt` never needs writes until `--commit`). All git invocations carry a 5 s timeout (configurable); timeout → git section marked `unavailable`, never a hang.
docs/plans/2026-07-11-baton-v1.md:64:**Transcript policy** (Gate-1 additions, iterations 1+2): transcript-tail capture at PreCompact is **opt-in and off by default** (`capture.transcriptTail` in `baton.config.json`), bounded (last 10 messages, 8 KB cap), passed through a secret-redaction filter (common credential/token patterns; fixtures with planted secrets prove redaction), stored in a dedicated bundle field excluded from HANDOFF.md by default. `baton purge-transcript` removes the field from **every retained copy** — active snapshot, `.bak`, journal (rewrite-and-rotate under lock), and all `history/` freezes — with a test asserting planted secrets are absent from the entire `.handoff/` tree afterward. **Diagnostics logging contract (iteration 4)**: `.handoff/log/` entries are metadata-only and redacted — raw hook payloads never enter them (fixture-proven) — with bounded retention (last 50 entries / 7 days) and inclusion in the whole-tree purge test.
docs/plans/2026-07-11-baton-v1.md:66:**Checkpoint engine**: adapters send pre-normalized `baton/event@1` on stdin (the real API); raw hook payloads get best-effort per-platform extraction (`normalize.mjs`) that never throws — unknowns degrade to `note` events. Idempotent via dedupe keys; journal append always, snapshot rewrite throttled (≥10 events / 30 s / important event types). **Hook-safety rule: exit 0 on all soft failures** so a checkpoint can never break the host harness (`--strict` for CI). `finalize --reason "…" [--reason-class] [--to]` records the switch reason (class inferred from text via the classifier when omitted), refreshes git state, freezes history.
docs/plans/2026-07-11-baton-v1.md:68:**Limit detector**: two tiers — **structured signals first** (Claude Code `StopFailure` matcher error types; documented limit-specific fields in codex JSON output), text signatures as fallback. **Exit-code rule (iteration 3): a generic nonzero exit never classifies as usage-limit on its own** — exit 10 requires limit-specific structured or text evidence; nonzero without evidence = `other-error`. Fixture corpus includes non-limit failing processes. Signatures are **versioned data** (`core/data/signatures.v1.json`), seeded with the verified strings for all three platforms, matcher kinds `substring`/`regex`/`json-field`, per-signature confidence, precedence usage-limit > auth > throttle > other-error, `--signatures <path>` overlay for hot-patching drift, `--explain` for auditability. Hardening (Gate-1 additions): input is ANSI-stripped before matching; the fixture corpus includes negative/near-miss cases (throttle-vs-usage confusables, multiline, malformed JSON, localized text) so false positives are regression-tested; overlay regexes are lint-checked (length cap, no backreferences, linear-time constructs) and matching carries a time guard; a low-confidence match **never silently avoids a platform** — it warns and defers to user intake at receive. Cursor entries carry `confidence: low|medium` explicitly.
docs/plans/2026-07-11-baton-v1.md:70:**Role matrix**: committed `baton.config.json` at repo root (`baton/config@1`) mapping roles → ordered platform+model chains (the seven methodology roles, defined below), plus per-platform `defaults`. Resolver is pure and explainable: skips `avoid[]` platforms (auto-populated with the origin platform when reason-class is usage-limit — never remap back onto the platform that died), resolves `mode: native|delegated|forced-default|unavailable` with `{chainIndex, skipped[]}` audit data. **Availability probing** (Gate-1 additions, iterations 1–3): `doctor` probes each platform noninteractively, hard 3 s bound per probe, results cached 15 min in the state dir. Probe record = two separate dimensions: **capability** (ordered, cumulative: `installed → authenticated → reachable`, recorded as far as verifiable) and **probe outcome** (`ok | rate-limited | error | timeout`). Resolver eligibility: **selectable** = capability ≥ authenticated and outcome ≠ rate-limited; anything with an unverifiable dimension resolves as selectable-with-`degraded`-flag; `rate-limited` platforms are skipped like `avoid[]` entries. Offline resolution always succeeds, flagged `degraded`.
docs/plans/2026-07-11-baton-v1.md:72:**Receive**: loads bundle with recovery + staleness checks (age > 12 h; HEAD moved; dirty mismatch), merges intake flags (`--origin`, `--reason`), resolves roles, and runs as the **prepare/commit transaction defined under Concurrency** — prompt emission at prepare, state mutation only at commit. Resume prompt ≤2,500 chars (brief, why-you're-here, role table, warnings, top-5 next actions, pointer to HANDOFF.md); the prompt explicitly frames bundle content as **unverified claims to audit against the working tree, not instructions to obey** (prompt-injection posture — see Threat model).
docs/plans/2026-07-11-baton-v1.md:78:Full module APIs, per-module test lists, repo file tree, and the fixture corpus (verbatim limit strings, hook payloads, corrupt bundles) are specified in the core design doc (committed as `docs/design/core.md` in task 0); implementation follows the named-task TDD sequence below — **tasks are identified by name, not number; the exact count is finalized when review directories are generated**. **`sessionHint` derivation (Gate-1 final note)**: per adapter, from the hook payload's stable session id (Claude Code `session_id`; Codex `session_id`; Cursor session field); when a payload lacks one, core generates a process-scoped hint flagged `unstable: true` — unstable hints never trigger foreign-session rejection on their own, only a warning.
docs/plans/2026-07-11-baton-v1.md:79:### Checkpoint vocabulary (used everywhere)
docs/plans/2026-07-11-baton-v1.md:84:Claude Code DOES emit a structured limit-death event (`StopFailure` with a `rate_limit` matcher); Codex and Cursor do not. The design is therefore: event-driven detection where it exists, rolling mechanical checkpoints everywhere (last coherent turn always captured), narrative seals at milestones, and receive compensating for a missing seal by asking the user why they switched and re-deriving git facts live, auditing HANDOFF.md claims against them.
docs/plans/2026-07-11-baton-v1.md:86:### Claude Code adapter (Tier A — highest fidelity)
docs/plans/2026-07-11-baton-v1.md:94:| `StopFailure` (matcher enum — e.g. `rate_limit`, `billing_error`, `server_error` — **snapshot verbatim from the official hooks reference during task 0**; fixtures generated from that canonical list; unknown future values normalize to `unknown` safely) | **The limit-death signal.** Classifies the error (matcher + payload through `baton detect`), writes a fail-open mechanical checkpoint stamped `trigger: stop-failure/<type>`, and on `rate_limit` marks the bundle limit-hit so the next SessionStart (any platform) surfaces it. Output/exit code are ignored by the harness — safe by construction. Tests cover the full snapshot enum. | 30 s |
docs/plans/2026-07-11-baton-v1.md:98:| `SessionStart` | Not a checkpoint: if a sealed or limit-hit bundle from another platform exists, inject "handoff pending — suggest `/baton:receive`" context | 10 s |
docs/plans/2026-07-11-baton-v1.md:104:### Codex adapter (Tier A− — hook-driven as of Codex >= 0.144)
docs/plans/2026-07-11-baton-v1.md:106:Gate-1 correction: Codex now ships lifecycle hooks, so the adapter is redesigned around them. Surfaces: (1) **repo `.codex/hooks.json`** (written by `init --codex`, merged never clobbered): `Stop` → mechanical checkpoint, `PreCompact` → context-boundary checkpoint, `SessionStart` → pending-handoff notice via injected context. No StopFailure equivalent exists on Codex, so a limit death is bounded by the last Stop checkpoint (one turn worst case). (2) `.agents/skills/baton-handoff/SKILL.md` per the Agent Skills standard — carries the receive/handoff protocol, invoked as `$baton-handoff` or by description match. (3) Repo `AGENTS.md` Handoff Protocol section as the always-on instruction layer (narrative checkpoints after subtasks / before risky ops / at wind-down). (4) `~/.codex/prompts/*.md` demoted to **legacy fallback only** (deprecated upstream), written only with `--with-legacy-prompts`. The notify shim is **cut** — hooks make it obsolete; `~/.codex/config.toml` is never touched. **Trust gate** (verified, Gate-1 iteration 2): Codex requires per-definition review of non-managed command hooks — trust is recorded against the hook's hash, and new/changed hooks are skipped until re-trusted via `/hooks`. Setup and doctor therefore report four distinct states per hook — installed / enabled / **trusted** / observed-executing (canary event) — and the fidelity claim is gated on the canary, with setup guiding the user through `/hooks` when review is pending. Hook definitions include `commandWindows` overrides. Fidelity statement ships verbatim in README + doctor: mechanical staleness ≤ 1 turn via Stop hook **once trusted**; narrative staleness bounded by protocol compliance; git state always re-derived at receive. Requires codex-cli >= 0.144 (doctor enforces; older versions fall back to protocol-only Tier C with an explicit warning).
docs/plans/2026-07-11-baton-v1.md:108:### Cursor adapter (Tier A− — hook-driven; event names verified against official docs, Gate-1 iteration 2)
docs/plans/2026-07-11-baton-v1.md:110:Surfaces: `.cursor/hooks.json` (project-level, `{"version":1,"hooks":{...}}`) with the **verified** events — `stop` → mechanical checkpoint per agent turn (same semantics as Claude Code `Stop`); `afterFileEdit` → debounced checkpoint (debounce enforced by the CLI, one real checkpoint per 2 min); `beforeShellExecution` with a git-commit matcher → checkpoint before agent-issued commits (**documented limitation: commits made through the SCM UI are invisible to hooks — no pre-commit event exists**); `sessionStart` → pending-handoff notice; `preCompact` → context-boundary checkpoint. Plus `.cursor/commands/{receive,handoff,baton-setup}.md` (CLI loading unconfirmed — convenience layer; every behavior also reachable via AGENTS.md, which cursor-agent reads natively, and via the universal fallback **`baton receive --print-prompt`**) and a ≤5-line `.cursor/rules/baton-handoff.mdc` (`alwaysApply: true`). hooks.json written only after explicit confirmation, merged not clobbered; project hooks require a **trusted workspace** — doctor checks trust and gates the fidelity claim on an observed hook canary. Fidelity: mechanical staleness ≤ 1 turn via `stop` when trusted; untrusted/cloud contexts degrade to protocol-only with an explicit warning.

docs/plans/2026-07-11-baton-v1.md:189:### Compatibility policy (Gate-1 addition)
docs/plans/2026-07-11-baton-v1.md:191:Tested version floors, enforced/reported by `doctor`: Claude Code ≥ the version documenting `StopFailure` (verify exact floor during task 0); codex-cli ≥ 0.144 (hooks; older → protocol-only Tier C with warning); cursor-agent ≥ 2026.05. Untested-newer versions get a doctor info line, not a failure. Signature table carries its own `updated` date; hooks payload drift is absorbed by the `baton/event@1` normalization seam.
docs/plans/2026-07-11-baton-v1.md:193:### Threat model (Gate-1 addition)
docs/plans/2026-07-11-baton-v1.md:195:Bundle, config, and overlay files are **untrusted inputs**: path fields from bundles are never honored as absolute paths or `..` traversals (normalized + repo-root-jailed); `.handoff/` entries that are symlinks are refused; overlay regexes are lint-gated (length, no backreferences, linear-time) with a match time guard; resume prompts frame bundle content as claims-to-audit, not instructions (mitigates a poisoned HANDOFF.md steering the receiving model); transcript capture is opt-in with secret redaction and purge. Dedicated test fixtures for each: traversal paths, symlinked bundle dir, pathological regex, planted secrets, adversarial HANDOFF.md.
docs/plans/2026-07-11-baton-v1.md:197:### Top risks (merged, deduplicated)
docs/plans/2026-07-11-baton-v1.md:202:4. **Partial-write corruption at the exact moment limits hit** — tmp+rename snapshots, `.bak`, journal replay; kill-mid-write is a first-class e2e test.
docs/plans/2026-07-11-baton-v1.md:204:6. **Cursor surface uncertainty** (commands loading unconfirmed, hooks need trusted workspace) — `--print-prompt` universal fallback; empirical verification before fidelity claims.
docs/plans/2026-07-11-baton-v1.md:207:## Out of scope for v1 (explicit seams left)
docs/plans/2026-07-11-baton-v1.md:213:## Gate-1 review record
docs/plans/2026-07-11-baton-v1.md:215:- **Iteration 1** (2026-07-11, gpt-5.6-sol @ xhigh, read-only `codex exec`): **BLOCKED** — 3 blocking (StopFailure hook missed; Codex hooks/prompts-deprecation missed; no bundle locking/concurrency), 6 major (build-order deps, receive atomicity, sandbox/root assumptions, journal rotation, detector false-positives, transcript privacy), 1 minor (self-referential gate claim; sole-author scoping). Blocking claims independently verified against official docs before incorporation.      1	// Threat-model guards (plan §Threat model): bundle-borne paths and the managed
     2	// .handoff tree are untrusted input. jailRelPath is the single pure gate every
     3	// repository path passes through before storage or render; checkHandoffTree
     4	// refuses to operate on a managed tree that contains symlinks or resolves
     5	// outside the repository root.
     6	
     7	const NUL = String.fromCharCode(0);
     8	
     9	/**
    10	 * Normalize an untrusted repository-relative path, or return null when it is
    11	 * unsafe: non-string, empty, NUL bytes, absolute (posix, Windows drive, UNC),
    12	 * or escaping the root via `..`. Backslashes are treated as separators so a
    13	 * Windows spelling cannot smuggle a traversal past a posix check.
    14	 * @param {any} p
    15	 * @returns {string | null}
    16	 */
    17	export function jailRelPath(p) {
    18	  if (typeof p !== 'string' || p.length === 0 || p.includes(NUL)) return null;
    19	  const unified = p.replace(/\\/g, '/');
    20	  if (unified.startsWith('/')) return null;
    21	  if (/^[A-Za-z]:/.test(unified)) return null;
    22	  /** @type {string[]} */
    23	  const out = [];
    24	  for (const seg of unified.split('/')) {
    25	    if (seg === '' || seg === '.') continue;
    26	    if (seg === '..') {
    27	      if (out.length === 0) return null;
    28	      out.pop();
    29	      continue;
    30	    }
    31	    out.push(seg);
    32	  }
    33	  if (out.length === 0) return null;
    34	  return out.join('/');
    35	}
    36	
    37	/** Thrown by store mutations when the managed tree fails the jail check. */
    38	export class UnsafeTreeError extends Error {}
    39	
    40	/**
    41	 * Verify the managed .handoff tree is safe to operate on: the directory itself
    42	 * is not a symlink, its realpath stays under the repository root, and no entry
    43	 * anywhere in the tree is a symlink (lstat walk — never follows links). An
    44	 * absent tree is safe; an unverifiable tree is refused, never trusted.
    45	 * @param {string} root @param {any} io
    46	 * @returns {{ok: true} | {ok: false, problem: string}}
    47	 */
    48	export function checkHandoffTree(root, io) {
    49	  const dir = `${root}/.handoff`;
    50	  try {
    51	    if (!io.fs.existsSync(dir)) return { ok: true };
    52	    if (io.fs.lstatSync(dir).isSymbolicLink()) {
    53	      return { ok: false, problem: `.handoff is a symlink — refusing to operate through a linked managed tree` };
    54	    }
    55	    const rootReal = io.fs.realpathSync(root);
    56	    const dirReal = io.fs.realpathSync(dir);
    57	    if (dirReal !== `${rootReal}/.handoff`) {
    58	      return { ok: false, problem: `.handoff resolves outside the repository root (${dirReal}) — refusing` };
    59	    }
    60	    /** @type {string[]} */
    61	    const stack = [dir];
    62	    while (stack.length > 0) {
    63	      const d = /** @type {string} */ (stack.pop());
    64	      for (const name of io.fs.readdirSync(d)) {
    65	        const p = `${d}/${name}`;
    66	        const st = io.fs.lstatSync(p);
    67	        if (st.isSymbolicLink()) return { ok: false, problem: `${p} is a symlink — refusing (managed-tree jail)` };
    68	        if (st.isDirectory()) stack.push(p);
    69	      }
    70	    }
    71	    return { ok: true };
    72	  } catch (err) {
    73	    const msg = /** @type {any} */ (err)?.message ?? String(err);
    74	    return { ok: false, problem: `managed tree could not be verified (${msg}) — refusing` };
    75	  }
    76	}
    77	
    78	/**
    79	 * Assert form of checkHandoffTree for mutation paths: throws UnsafeTreeError
    80	 * so commands surface the refusal through their existing soft-failure handling.
    81	 * @param {string} root @param {any} io
    82	 */
    83	export function assertHandoffTreeSafe(root, io) {
    84	  const r = checkHandoffTree(root, io);
    85	  if (!r.ok) throw new UnsafeTreeError(r.problem);
    86	}
     1	import { Worker } from 'node:worker_threads';
     2	
     3	// Load-time pathological-regex probe (plan §Threat model; gate-2 fix 6). The
     4	// structural lint in signatures.mjs rejects every quantified-group shape it
     5	// can see, but a pattern with no groups at all (e.g. a chained-star `a*a*…x`)
     6	// can still backtrack combinatorially. This probe runs the candidate against
     7	// adversarial inputs inside a worker thread and enforces a hard wall-clock
     8	// deadline via Atomics.wait — a synchronous regex match cannot be interrupted
     9	// in-process, so isolation is the only reliable bound. Overlay tables pass
    10	// through this at load; the classify() hot path stays synchronous because
    11	// every table it sees was probed here first.
    12	
    13	const DEADLINE_MS = 1000; // generous vs worker spawn (~50-100 ms); tiny vs the 93.8 s repro
    14	const INPUT_LEN = 4096;
    15	
    16	/**
    17	 * Build adversarial probe inputs from the pattern itself: long runs of the
    18	 * pattern's own literal characters ending in a byte that cannot match, which
    19	 * forces a full backtrack on pathological shapes.
    20	 * @param {string} pattern
    21	 * @returns {string[]}
    22	 */
    23	function probeInputs(pattern) {
    24	  const chars = [...new Set(pattern.match(/[A-Za-z0-9]/g) ?? [])].slice(0, 4);
    25	  if (chars.length === 0) chars.push('a');
    26	  const tail = String.fromCharCode(1);
    27	  const inputs = chars.map((c) => c.repeat(INPUT_LEN) + tail);
    28	  inputs.push('a'.repeat(INPUT_LEN) + tail);
    29	  return inputs;
    30	}
    31	
    32	/**
    33	 * @param {string} pattern @param {string} [flags] @param {number} [timeoutMs]
    34	 * @returns {{safe: true} | {safe: false, reason: string}}
    35	 */
    36	export function probeRegexSafe(pattern, flags = '', timeoutMs = DEADLINE_MS) {
    37	  const sab = new SharedArrayBuffer(4);
    38	  const flag = new Int32Array(sab);
    39	  const worker = new Worker(new URL('./probe-worker.mjs', import.meta.url), {
    40	    workerData: { pattern, flags, inputs: probeInputs(pattern), sab },
    41	  });
    42	  const waited = Atomics.wait(flag, 0, 0, timeoutMs);
    43	  worker.terminate();
    44	  worker.unref();
    45	  if (waited === 'timed-out') {
    46	    return { safe: false, reason: `pathological backtracking: probe match exceeded the ${timeoutMs} ms deadline` };
    47	  }
    48	  return { safe: true };
    49	}
     1	// Worker side of the regex pathological probe (see probe.mjs). Runs the
     2	// candidate pattern against adversarial inputs in an isolated thread; the main
     3	// thread waits on the shared flag with a hard deadline and terminates this
     4	// worker if the matches never finish. flag[0]: 0 = running, 1 = done.
     5	import { workerData } from 'node:worker_threads';
     6	
     7	const { pattern, flags, inputs, sab } = workerData;
     8	const flag = new Int32Array(sab);
     9	
    10	try {
    11	  const re = new RegExp(pattern, flags);
    12	  for (const s of inputs) re.test(s);
    13	} catch {
    14	  // A compile error is not a hang; the lint layer reports syntax separately.
    15	}
    16	
    17	Atomics.store(flag, 0, 1);
    18	Atomics.notify(flag, 0);
     1	import { loadBundle } from '../bundle/store.mjs';
     2	import { parseFlags, resolveRoot } from './shared.mjs';
     3	
     4	// Per-platform receive pointers and context shapes (plan §adapters; gate-2
     5	// fix 8). Claude Code injects hookSpecificOutput.additionalContext, Cursor
     6	// expects additional_context JSON, Codex surfaces plain hook stdout.
     7	/** @type {Record<string, string>} */
     8	const RESUME_HINT = {
     9	  'claude-code': 'Suggest running /baton:receive to resume that task with remapped roles.',
    10	  codex: "Run 'baton receive --platform codex' or the baton-handoff skill to resume that task with remapped roles.",
    11	  cursor: "Run 'baton receive --platform cursor' to resume that task with remapped roles.",
    12	};
    13	
    14	/**
    15	 * `baton session-start` — the cheap SessionStart gate the Codex and Cursor
    16	 * adapters call instead of full receive preparation (gate-2 fix 8). Inspects
    17	 * only pending-foreign state: a bundle that is sealed OR limit-hit AND from
    18	 * another platform yields a harness-shaped notice on stdout; every other state
    19	 * — own bundle, open non-limit bundle, no bundle, corrupt tree — is a quiet
    20	 * no-op. ALWAYS exits 0: a session-start notice must never break a session.
    21	 * @param {string[]} args @param {any} io
    22	 * @returns {Promise<number>}
    23	 */
    24	export async function cmdSessionStart(args, io) {
    25	  try {
    26	    const { flags } = parseFlags(args);
    27	    const platform = typeof flags.platform === 'string' ? flags.platform : null;
    28	    if (!platform) {
    29	      io.stderr.write('baton session-start: --platform <claude-code|codex|cursor> is required — emitting nothing\n');
    30	      return 0;
    31	    }
    32	
    33	    const root = resolveRoot(io, flags);
    34	    // Full load (journal replay included): an unsealed limit death may live
    35	    // only as a post-snapshot journal note. Load warnings are deliberately
    36	    // not surfaced — this is a notice path, not a diagnostics path.
    37	    const { bundle } = loadBundle(root, io);
    38	    if (bundle === null) return 0;
    39	
    40	    const origin = bundle?.origin?.platform;
    41	    const pending = bundle?.handoff?.status === 'sealed' || bundle?.handoff?.reasonClass === 'usage-limit';
    42	    const foreign = typeof origin === 'string' && origin !== platform;
    43	    if (!pending || !foreign) return 0;
    44	
    45	    const kind = bundle.handoff.status === 'sealed' ? 'sealed' : 'limit-hit before sealing';
    46	    const msg = `A handoff bundle from ${origin} is pending in .handoff/ (${kind}). ${RESUME_HINT[platform] ?? RESUME_HINT.codex}`;
    47	
    48	    if (platform === 'cursor') {
    49	      io.stdout.write(JSON.stringify({ additional_context: msg }) + '\n');
    50	    } else if (platform === 'claude-code') {
    51	      io.stdout.write(JSON.stringify({ hookSpecificOutput: { hookEventName: 'SessionStart', additionalContext: msg } }) + '\n');
    52	    } else {
    53	      io.stdout.write(msg + '\n');
    54	    }
    55	    return 0;
    56	  } catch {
    57	    return 0;
    58	  }
    59	}
     1	import { recoverLock } from '../bundle/lock.mjs';
     2	import { emitEnvelope, parseFlags, resolveRoot } from './shared.mjs';
     3	
     4	/**
     5	 * `baton recover` — explicit lock recovery (gate-2 fix 11: this sat in the
     6	 * frozen command list unimplemented). Exactly ONE state recovers: a
     7	 * provably-dead same-host owner. `--force` is an intent flag only — live,
     8	 * cross-host, and torn-metadata locks refuse with the same reasons either way
     9	 * (plan §Concurrency: never steal on uncertainty).
    10	 * @param {string[]} args @param {any} io
    11	 * @returns {Promise<number>}
    12	 */
    13	export async function cmdRecover(args, io) {
    14	  const { flags } = parseFlags(args);
    15	  const root = resolveRoot(io, flags);
    16	  const r = recoverLock(root, io, { force: flags.force === true });
    17	
    18	  if (r.recovered) {
    19	    if (flags.json) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: true, data: { recovered: true } });
    20	    else io.stdout.write('baton recover: lock recovered (or already free) — nothing holds .handoff/lock\n');
    21	    return 0;
    22	  }
    23	  if (flags.json) {
    24	    emitEnvelope(io, { ok: false, data: { recovered: false }, error: { code: 'refused', msg: r.refusedReason ?? 'refused' } });
    25	  } else {
    26	    io.stderr.write(`baton recover: ${r.refusedReason}\n`);
    27	  }
    28	  return 1;
    29	}
     1	import { loadBundle } from '../bundle/store.mjs';
     2	import { emitEnvelope, parseFlags, resolveRoot } from './shared.mjs';
     3	
     4	/**
     5	 * `baton status` — report the active bundle THROUGH the recovery ladder
     6	 * (gate-2 minor 16: the old inline version read bundle.json raw, so a corrupt
     7	 * snapshot with a perfectly good .bak reported failure). Load warnings go to
     8	 * stderr; --json carries them in the envelope.
     9	 * @param {string[]} args @param {any} io
    10	 * @returns {Promise<number>}
    11	 */
    12	export async function cmdStatus(args, io) {
    13	  const { flags } = parseFlags(args);
    14	  const root = resolveRoot(io, flags);
    15	  const { bundle, warnings } = loadBundle(root, io);
    16	  for (const w of warnings) io.stderr.write(`baton status: ${w}\n`);
    17	
    18	  if (bundle === null) {
    19	    const error = { code: 'no-bundle', msg: `no handoff bundle at ${root}/.handoff/bundle.json; run 'baton init' or let checkpoints create one` };
    20	    if (flags.json) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: false, error, warnings });
    21	    else io.stderr.write(`baton status: ${error.msg}\n`);
    22	    return 1;
    23	  }
    24	
    25	  const data = {
    26	    bundleId: bundle.bundleId ?? null,
    27	    status: bundle.handoff?.status ?? null,
    28	    generation: bundle.generation ?? null,
    29	    updatedAt: bundle.updatedAt ?? null,
    30	  };
    31	  if (flags.json) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: true, data, warnings });
    32	  else io.stdout.write(`bundle ${data.bundleId ?? '?'} — ${data.status ?? '?'} — generation ${data.generation ?? '?'} — updated ${data.updatedAt ?? '?'}\n`);
    33	  return 0;
    34	}
     1	import { safeReadJson } from '../util/fsx.mjs';
     2	
     3	// Doctor's probe results live in .handoff/log/probe-cache.json for 15 minutes
     4	// (plan §Availability probing). This reader is the consumption side (gate-2
     5	// major 13): remap's resolver and receive's token derivation take the FRESH
     6	// cache; a stale or absent cache reads as null — offline resolution stays
     7	// possible, flagged degraded by the resolver.
     8	
     9	export const PROBE_CACHE_MS = 15 * 60 * 1000;
    10	
    11	/**
    12	 * @param {string} root @param {any} io
    13	 * @returns {Record<string, {capability: string, outcome: string}> | null}
    14	 */
    15	export function readProbeCache(root, io) {
    16	  const cached = safeReadJson(io.fs, `${root}/.handoff/log/probe-cache.json`);
    17	  if (!cached.ok || typeof cached.value?.at !== 'string' || !Array.isArray(cached.value.records)) return null;
    18	  if (!(Date.parse(io.now()) - Date.parse(cached.value.at) < PROBE_CACHE_MS)) return null;
    19	  /** @type {Record<string, {capability: string, outcome: string}>} */
    20	  const out = {};
    21	  for (const r of cached.value.records) {
    22	    // capability null in a probe record means the binary was not found — a
    23	    // VERIFIED not-installed, which the resolver must skip, not select.
    24	    if (r && typeof r.platform === 'string') out[r.platform] = { capability: r.capability ?? 'not-installed', outcome: r.outcome ?? 'error' };
    25	  }
    26	  return out;
    27	}
     1	// Secret redaction for transcript tails (plan §Transcript policy; gate-2
     2	// fix 10). Pattern classes cover the common credential shapes; anything
     3	// matched is replaced wholesale with [redacted]. False positives are cheap
     4	// (a redacted transcript line), false negatives are not — patterns lean broad.
     5	
     6	const MARKER = '[redacted]';
     7	
     8	const PATTERNS = [
     9	  // PEM private key blocks (multi-line) — first so inner matches don't shred it.
    10	  /-----BEGIN [A-Z ]*PRIVATE KEY-----[\s\S]*?-----END [A-Z ]*PRIVATE KEY-----/g,
    11	  // Vendor token prefixes.
    12	  /\bsk-[A-Za-z0-9_-]{8,}/g, // OpenAI / Anthropic (incl. sk-ant-…)
    13	  /\bAKIA[0-9A-Z]{16}\b/g, // AWS access key id
    14	  /\b(?:ghp|gho|ghu|ghs|ghr)_[A-Za-z0-9]{20,}\b/g, // GitHub tokens
    15	  /\bgithub_pat_[A-Za-z0-9_]{20,}\b/g,
    16	  /\bxox[baprs]-[A-Za-z0-9-]{10,}\b/g, // Slack
    17	  // JWTs (three base64url segments, first decoding to a JSON header).
    18	  /\bey[A-Za-z0-9_-]{8,}\.[A-Za-z0-9_-]{4,}\.[A-Za-z0-9_-]{4,}\b/g,
    19	  // Authorization headers.
    20	  /\bBearer\s+[A-Za-z0-9._~+/=-]{16,}/g,
    21	  // Generic credential assignments: api_key = "…", password: …, token=…
    22	  /\b(?:api[_-]?key|secret|token|passwd|password|authorization)\b\s*[:=]\s*["']?[^\s"']{8,}["']?/gi,
    23	];
    24	
    25	/**
    26	 * @param {string} text
    27	 * @returns {string}
    28	 */
    29	export function redactSecrets(text) {
    30	  if (typeof text !== 'string' || text.length === 0) return typeof text === 'string' ? text : '';
    31	  let out = text;
    32	  for (const re of PATTERNS) out = out.replace(re, MARKER);
    33	  return out;
    34	}

7:Saksham's AI-coding workflow spans three harnesses — Claude Code, OpenAI Codex CLI, and Cursor — and breaks whenever one platform's usage limits run out mid-task. Work state, plan context, and the writer/reviewer role assignments are trapped in the dying session. His handwritten notes describe a three-layer system (ideal-loop creator with smoke-test gate; dual-worktree adversarial write/review pipeline; cross-platform workflow portability). **Milestone 1 (this plan): the cross-platform failover layer** — chosen by Saksham as the foundation, since it addresses the stated pain point and the other layers run on top of it.

42:- **Fable 5** (platform.claude.com guide): strong instruction-following — brief steering beats enumeration; state boundaries (pause only for destructive/irreversible/scope-change/user-only input); "when you have enough information to act, act"; audit progress claims against tool evidence; parallel subagents with async communication; provide a memory/notes location; fresh-context verifier subagents beat self-critique; over-prescriptive skills degrade output; never instruct reasoning echo (triggers refusals).
56:**Bundle** (`.handoff/`): single active bundle = `bundle.json` (atomic tmp+rename snapshot, `.bak` of previous good) + `journal.ndjson` (append-only events, torn-tail tolerant) + rendered `HANDOFF.md` + `history/` (unified retention: last 10 entries **per type** — finalize freezes, takeover archives, receive archives — each = frozen snapshot plus rotated journal; receipts needed for replay/audit are always preserved within retained entries). Load = snapshot + replay journal entries past `journalSeq`; recovery paths: `.bak` + replay, or full journal rebuild. Schema `baton/bundle@1`: origin platform/model, task goal/constraints/acceptance, plan steps with status, capped decisions log, files touched, role assignments, git branch/HEAD/dirty summary, handoff finalize block (`status: open|sealed|received` + `receive_log[]`), compaction markers. Size discipline is **deterministic truncation only** (decisions capped first-20+last-150 with marker; no LLM summarization).
58:**Concurrency** (Gate-1 additions, iterations 1+2): all bundle mutations run under a repo-scoped lock — `.handoff/lock` acquired via atomic `mkdir`/`O_EXCL`, containing `{writerId: <platform>-<pid>-<sessionHint>, fencingToken: <random per acquisition>, acquiredAt, heartbeatAt}`. **Lock model (iterations 3+4)**: mutation locks are **operation-scoped** — acquired for the duration of one command's mutation and released, never held across turns; logical session ownership lives in `bundle.json` (the active `origin`/generation), not in the lock. Lock metadata (host, pid, process start time, fencing token) is written atomically with acquisition; a crash between lock creation and metadata publication reads as torn metadata. Recovery rules, conservative on every OS: automatic recovery only when the owner is **provably dead** (same host, pid gone or pid/start-time mismatch); a same-host owner confirmed alive is **refused** — including `recover --force` — with the instruction to terminate that process first; missing/torn/cross-host/unverifiable owner metadata is treated as **unsupported for automatic recovery** (refusal with explanation), never as safe-to-steal. Fencing tokens remain a fast-abort layer. Tests: force attempted against a live paused writer (refused), crash between lock creation and metadata publication, owner paused after its final token check, torn-metadata refusal. Journal entries carry `writerId`; sequence numbers are allocated under the lock. **Semantic isolation**: v1 enforces one active task — a checkpoint whose `sessionHint`/origin doesn't match the active bundle is rejected with an actionable warning (`--take-over` supersedes: archives the current bundle to `history/` and starts fresh), so two unrelated sessions can't merge goals/files/decisions into one handoff. `receive` is a core-owned two-phase transaction. `receive --prepare` validates + resolves roles + returns a one-time receipt token and the resume prompt without mutating state; the token **binds** the bundle revision (journalSeq), normalized intake, config digest, target platform+session, the probe snapshot used for remap, **and a content-sensitive prepare-time git digest** — HEAD + branch + hashes of the staged and unstaged diffs + a bounded untracked-files digest (path + size + content hash, capped) — so a file that stays `M` but changes content still invalidates the token. `--commit` re-derives and compares every bound input, rejecting any drift (intervening checkpoint, config edit, HEAD move, content change, probe change) and requiring re-prepare; each declared drift input has its own rejection test. `receive --commit <token>` performs one atomic transition: `sealed → received` (normal) or `open → received` (the primary failure case: limit death before finalize; commit writes a receive-generated degraded seal recording intake origin/reason, flagged `degraded-seal`) — and in the same transition **adopts the receiving platform/session as the bundle's active owner and opens a fresh writable generation** (`generation` counter incremented, `status: open`), archiving the prior seal + receipt to `history/`. The receiving session's subsequent checkpoints are therefore first-party, and the bundle is immediately receivable again if the new platform also dies — chained A→B→C failover is contract-tested with checkpoints fired immediately after each receive. Cancellation or a competing receiver leaves state untouched; double-commit fails cleanly. Identical semantics on every adapter; six-direction contract tests cover sealed + unsealed variants. Tests: concurrent writers, provably-dead takeover, fencing-token abort incl. pause-after-final-check, foreign-session rejection/take-over, prepare-vs-checkpoint races, prepare-without-commit, double-commit, competing receivers, chained failovers.
66:**Checkpoint engine**: adapters send pre-normalized `baton/event@1` on stdin (the real API); raw hook payloads get best-effort per-platform extraction (`normalize.mjs`) that never throws — unknowns degrade to `note` events. Idempotent via dedupe keys; journal append always, snapshot rewrite throttled (≥10 events / 30 s / important event types). **Hook-safety rule: exit 0 on all soft failures** so a checkpoint can never break the host harness (`--strict` for CI). `finalize --reason "…" [--reason-class] [--to]` records the switch reason (class inferred from text via the classifier when omitted), refreshes git state, freezes history.
68:**Limit detector**: two tiers — **structured signals first** (Claude Code `StopFailure` matcher error types; documented limit-specific fields in codex JSON output), text signatures as fallback. **Exit-code rule (iteration 3): a generic nonzero exit never classifies as usage-limit on its own** — exit 10 requires limit-specific structured or text evidence; nonzero without evidence = `other-error`. Fixture corpus includes non-limit failing processes. Signatures are **versioned data** (`core/data/signatures.v1.json`), seeded with the verified strings for all three platforms, matcher kinds `substring`/`regex`/`json-field`, per-signature confidence, precedence usage-limit > auth > throttle > other-error, `--signatures <path>` overlay for hot-patching drift, `--explain` for auditability. Hardening (Gate-1 additions): input is ANSI-stripped before matching; the fixture corpus includes negative/near-miss cases (throttle-vs-usage confusables, multiline, malformed JSON, localized text) so false positives are regression-tested; overlay regexes are lint-checked (length cap, no backreferences, linear-time constructs) and matching carries a time guard; a low-confidence match **never silently avoids a platform** — it warns and defers to user intake at receive. Cursor entries carry `confidence: low|medium` explicitly.
70:**Role matrix**: committed `baton.config.json` at repo root (`baton/config@1`) mapping roles → ordered platform+model chains (the seven methodology roles, defined below), plus per-platform `defaults`. Resolver is pure and explainable: skips `avoid[]` platforms (auto-populated with the origin platform when reason-class is usage-limit — never remap back onto the platform that died), resolves `mode: native|delegated|forced-default|unavailable` with `{chainIndex, skipped[]}` audit data. **Availability probing** (Gate-1 additions, iterations 1–3): `doctor` probes each platform noninteractively, hard 3 s bound per probe, results cached 15 min in the state dir. Probe record = two separate dimensions: **capability** (ordered, cumulative: `installed → authenticated → reachable`, recorded as far as verifiable) and **probe outcome** (`ok | rate-limited | error | timeout`). Resolver eligibility: **selectable** = capability ≥ authenticated and outcome ≠ rate-limited; anything with an unverifiable dimension resolves as selectable-with-`degraded`-flag; `rate-limited` platforms are skipped like `avoid[]` entries. Offline resolution always succeeds, flagged `degraded`.
81:- **Mechanical checkpoint** (`baton checkpoint`): hook/script-driven, no LLM — git branch/HEAD/dirty summary, timestamps, source platform/model, files touched. <1 s, safe per-turn.

     1	import { probeRegexSafe } from './probe.mjs';
     2	
     3	const PLATFORMS = ['claude-code', 'codex', 'cursor'];
     4	const CLASSES = ['usage-limit', 'auth', 'throttle', 'other-error'];
     5	const MATCHER_KINDS = ['substring', 'regex', 'json-field'];
     6	const REGEX_MAX_LEN = 200;
     7	
     8	/**
     9	 * Is a quantified group's body ambiguous? Alternation, another quantifier, or
    10	 * any nested group inside a `(…)+`/`(…)*`/`(…){` makes backtracking
    11	 * super-linear in the worst case — conservatively rejected (gate-2 fix 6:
    12	 * the old lint accepted `(a|aa)+$` and the `(a|a)*x` live repro).
    13	 * @param {string} body
    14	 */
    15	function riskyGroupBody(body) {
    16	  // Strip a non-capture/lookaround/named marker so `(?:abc)+` reads as `abc`.
    17	  const b = body.replace(/^\?(?:<=|<!|:|=|!|<[^>]*>)/, '');
    18	  let inClass = false;
    19	  for (let i = 0; i < b.length; i++) {
    20	    const c = b[i];
    21	    if (c === '\\') {
    22	      i++;
    23	      continue;
    24	    }
    25	    if (inClass) {
    26	      if (c === ']') inClass = false;
    27	      continue;
    28	    }
    29	    if (c === '[') {
    30	      inClass = true;
    31	      continue;
    32	    }
    33	    if (c === '|' || c === '+' || c === '*' || c === '{' || c === '?' || c === '(' || c === ')') return true;
    34	  }
    35	  return false;
    36	}
    37	
    38	/**
    39	 * Structural scan: walk the pattern (class- and escape-aware), and for every
    40	 * group followed by an unbounded quantifier check its full body — any nesting
    41	 * depth — for ambiguity. Returns a problem string or null.
    42	 * @param {string} pattern
    43	 */
    44	function scanQuantifiedGroups(pattern) {
    45	  /** @type {number[]} */
    46	  const opens = [];
    47	  let inClass = false;
    48	  for (let i = 0; i < pattern.length; i++) {
    49	    const c = pattern[i];
    50	    if (c === '\\') {
    51	      i++;
    52	      continue;
    53	    }
    54	    if (inClass) {
    55	      if (c === ']') inClass = false;
    56	      continue;
    57	    }
    58	    if (c === '[') {
    59	      inClass = true;
    60	      continue;
    61	    }
    62	    if (c === '(') {
    63	      opens.push(i);
    64	      continue;
    65	    }
    66	    if (c === ')') {
    67	      const start = opens.pop();
    68	      if (start === undefined) continue; // unbalanced — new RegExp() reports it
    69	      const next = pattern[i + 1];
    70	      if (next === '+' || next === '*' || next === '{') {
    71	        const body = pattern.slice(start + 1, i);
    72	        if (riskyGroupBody(body)) {
    73	          return `quantified group "(${body})" contains alternation, another quantifier, or a nested group`;
    74	        }
    75	      }
    76	    }
    77	  }
    78	  return null;
    79	}
    80	
    81	/**
    82	 * Lint one regex pattern. Together with the load-time worker probe on overlay
    83	 * tables (see probe.mjs), the runtime time-guard is by construction: patterns
    84	 * that could backtrack catastrophically never reach classify(), which stays
    85	 * synchronous with no timers.
    86	 * @param {string} id @param {string} pattern
    87	 */
    88	function lintRegex(id, pattern) {
    89	  if (typeof pattern !== 'string' || pattern.length > REGEX_MAX_LEN) {
    90	    throw new Error(`signature ${id}: regex pattern exceeds the ${REGEX_MAX_LEN}-char length cap (or is not a string)`);
    91	  }
    92	  if (/\\[1-9]/.test(pattern)) {
    93	    throw new Error(`signature ${id}: regex backreferences are not allowed`);
    94	  }
    95	  const problem = scanQuantifiedGroups(pattern);
    96	  if (problem) {
    97	    throw new Error(`signature ${id}: ${problem} (pathological backtracking / non-linear-time risk)`);
    98	  }
    99	  try {
   100	    // eslint-disable-next-line no-new
   101	    new RegExp(pattern);
   102	  } catch (err) {
   103	    throw new Error(`signature ${id}: invalid regex pattern (${/** @type {any} */ (err)?.message ?? 'syntax error'})`);
   104	  }
   105	}
   106	
   107	/** @param {any} s */
   108	function lintSignature(s) {
   109	  if (typeof s.id !== 'string' || s.id.length === 0) throw new Error('signature with missing id');
   110	  if (!PLATFORMS.includes(s.platform)) {
   111	    throw new Error(`signature ${s.id}: unknown platform "${s.platform}" (expected ${PLATFORMS.join('|')})`);
   112	  }
   113	  if (!CLASSES.includes(s.class)) throw new Error(`signature ${s.id}: unknown class "${s.class}"`);
   114	  if (!s.matcher || !MATCHER_KINDS.includes(s.matcher.kind)) {
   115	    throw new Error(`signature ${s.id}: unknown matcher kind "${s.matcher?.kind}" (expected ${MATCHER_KINDS.join('|')})`);
   116	  }
   117	  if (s.matcher.kind === 'regex') lintRegex(s.id, s.matcher.pattern);
   118	  if (s.matcher.kind === 'substring' && typeof s.matcher.value !== 'string') {
   119	    throw new Error(`signature ${s.id}: substring matcher needs a string value`);
   120	  }
   121	  if (s.matcher.kind === 'json-field' && typeof s.matcher.path !== 'string') {
   122	    throw new Error(`signature ${s.id}: json-field matcher needs a dotted string path`);
   123	  }
   124	  if (s.resetHint !== undefined) lintRegex(s.id, s.resetHint);
   125	}
   126	
   127	/** @param {any} fs @param {string} path @param {{probe?: boolean}} [opts] */
   128	function readTable(fs, path, opts = {}) {
   129	  const raw = fs.readFileSync(path, 'utf8');
   130	  const table = JSON.parse(raw);
   131	  if (table?.schema !== 'baton/signatures@1') {
   132	    throw new Error(`signature table at ${path}: expected schema baton/signatures@1, got ${JSON.stringify(table?.schema)}`);
   133	  }
   134	  if (!Array.isArray(table.signatures)) throw new Error(`signature table at ${path}: signatures must be an array`);
   135	  for (const s of table.signatures) {
   136	    lintSignature(s);
   137	    // Overlay tables are untrusted: the structural lint cannot see group-free
   138	    // pathological shapes (chained stars), so every overlay regex additionally
   139	    // runs against adversarial inputs in a worker with a hard deadline.
   140	    if (opts.probe === true) {
   141	      for (const pattern of [s.matcher?.kind === 'regex' ? s.matcher.pattern : null, s.resetHint ?? null]) {
   142	        if (typeof pattern !== 'string') continue;
   143	        const verdict = probeRegexSafe(pattern, s.matcher?.flags ?? '');
   144	        if (!verdict.safe) throw new Error(`signature ${s.id}: ${verdict.reason}`);
   145	      }
   146	    }
   147	  }
   148	  return table;
   149	}
   150	
   151	/**
   152	 * Load + validate the signature table, merging an optional overlay by id
   153	 * (later wins — the drift-absorbing hot-patch surface). Builtin patterns are
   154	 * package-shipped and statically linted; overlay patterns are additionally
   155	 * probe-guarded at load.
   156	 * @param {{builtinPath: string, overlayPath?: string}} paths @param {any} io
   157	 */
   158	export function loadSignatures({ builtinPath, overlayPath }, io) {
   159	  const base = readTable(io.fs, builtinPath);
   160	  if (!overlayPath) return base;
   161	
   162	  const overlay = readTable(io.fs, overlayPath, { probe: true });
   163	  const merged = [...base.signatures];
   164	  for (const s of overlay.signatures) {
   165	    const i = merged.findIndex((b) => b.id === s.id);
   166	    if (i === -1) merged.push(s);
   167	    else merged[i] = s;
   168	  }
   169	  return { ...base, updated: overlay.updated ?? base.updated, signatures: merged };
   170	}
     1	import { atomicWriteJson } from '../util/fsx.mjs';
     2	import { appendEntry, readAllTolerant } from '../util/jsonl.mjs';
     3	import { dedupeKey } from '../util/ids.mjs';
     4	
     5	/**
     6	 * Operation-scoped mutation lock (plan §Concurrency, Lock model).
     7	 * Acquire = atomic non-recursive mkdir of .handoff/lock/; exclusivity comes from
     8	 * EEXIST. Ownership facts live in owner.json; logical session ownership lives in
     9	 * bundle.json, never here. Takeover only when the owner is provably dead on this
    10	 * host — every other contended state refuses, and `force` never changes that.
    11	 */
    12	
    13	export class LockHeldError extends Error {
    14	  constructor(/** @type {string} */ message) {
    15	    super(message);
    16	    this.name = 'LockHeldError';
    17	  }
    18	}
    19	
    20	export class FencingError extends Error {
    21	  constructor(/** @type {string} */ message) {
    22	    super(message);
    23	    this.name = 'FencingError';
    24	  }
    25	}
    26	
    27	/** @param {string} root */
    28	function lockPaths(root) {
    29	  const dir = `${root}/.handoff`;
    30	  return {
    31	    dir,
    32	    lockDir: `${dir}/lock`,
    33	    owner: `${dir}/lock/owner.json`,
    34	    journal: `${dir}/journal.ndjson`,
    35	  };
    36	}
    37	
    38	const ESCAPE_HATCH =
    39	  'Verify manually that no baton process still holds it, then delete .handoff/lock to clear.';
    40	
    41	/**
    42	 * Inspect the current lock state.
    43	 * @param {any} io @param {ReturnType<typeof lockPaths>} p
    44	 * @returns {{state: 'free'} | {state: 'torn', cause: string} | {state: 'live'|'dead'|'cross-host', owner: any}}
    45	 */
    46	function inspect(io, p) {
    47	  if (!io.fs.existsSync(p.lockDir)) return { state: 'free' };
    48	  if (!io.fs.existsSync(p.owner)) {
    49	    return { state: 'torn', cause: 'owner.json is missing (torn lock metadata)' };
    50	  }
    51	  let owner;
    52	  try {
    53	    owner = JSON.parse(io.fs.readFileSync(p.owner, 'utf8'));
    54	  } catch {
    55	    return { state: 'torn', cause: 'owner.json is unparseable (torn lock metadata)' };
    56	  }
    57	  if (owner.host !== io.host) return { state: 'cross-host', owner };
    58	  if (io.processAlive(owner.pid, owner.startTime)) return { state: 'live', owner };
    59	  return { state: 'dead', owner };
    60	}
    61	
    62	/** @param {any} io @param {ReturnType<typeof lockPaths>} p @param {string} text */
    63	function journalNote(io, p, text) {
    64	  io.fs.mkdirSync(p.dir, { recursive: true });
    65	  // Allocate seq + dedupeKey like every other journal entry (gate-2 fix,
    66	  // reviewer-b finding 2): a keyless, seq-less note poisons journalSeq to NaN
    67	  // on replay and dedupes every later keyless event. We are already inside the
    68	  // lock here, so reading the tail for allocation is race-free.
    69	  let tailSeq = 0;
    70	  try {
    71	    const snap = JSON.parse(io.fs.readFileSync(`${p.dir}/bundle.json`, 'utf8'));
    72	    if (typeof snap?.journalSeq === 'number') tailSeq = snap.journalSeq;
    73	  } catch {
    74	    // no snapshot yet — journal tail below still counts
    75	  }
    76	  try {
    77	    for (const e of readAllTolerant(io.fs, p.journal).entries) {
    78	      if (typeof e.seq === 'number' && e.seq > tailSeq) tailSeq = e.seq;
    79	    }
    80	  } catch {
    81	    // no journal yet
    82	  }
    83	  const ts = io.now();
    84	  appendEntry(io.fs, p.journal, {
    85	    seq: tailSeq + 1,
    86	    ts,
    87	    type: 'note',
    88	    dedupeKey: dedupeKey({ ts, type: 'note', source: 'lock', text }),
    89	    writerId: ['lock', io.host, io.pid].join('-'),
    90	    source: 'lock',
    91	    payload: { text },
    92	  });
    93	}
    94	
    95	/** @param {any} io @param {ReturnType<typeof lockPaths>} p */
    96	function publishOwner(io, p) {
    97	  const token = io.newFencingToken();
    98	  atomicWriteJson(io.fs, p.owner, {
    99	    host: io.host,
   100	    pid: io.pid,
   101	    startTime: io.startTime,
   102	    fencingToken: token,
   103	    acquiredAt: io.now(),
   104	    heartbeatAt: io.now(),
   105	  });
   106	  return token;
   107	}
   108	
   109	/** @param {any} io @param {ReturnType<typeof lockPaths>} p */
   110	function acquire(io, p) {
   111	  io.fs.mkdirSync(p.dir, { recursive: true });
   112	  try {
   113	    io.fs.mkdirSync(p.lockDir);
   114	    return publishOwner(io, p);
   115	  } catch (err) {
   116	    if (/** @type {any} */ (err)?.code !== 'EEXIST') throw err;
   117	  }
   118	
   119	  const s = inspect(io, p);
   120	  switch (s.state) {
   121	    case 'free':
   122	      // Raced a release between mkdir failure and inspection; retry once. A
   123	      // competitor can win THIS mkdir too (gate-2 fix 12: observed under real
   124	      // barrier-released contention) — that is contention, not corruption.
   125	      try {
   126	        io.fs.mkdirSync(p.lockDir);
   127	      } catch (err) {
   128	        if (/** @type {any} */ (err)?.code === 'EEXIST') {
   129	          throw new LockHeldError('lock was re-acquired by a competitor mid-inspection — transient contention');
   130	        }
   131	        throw err;
   132	      }
   133	      return publishOwner(io, p);
   134	    case 'live':
   135	      throw new LockHeldError(
   136	        `lock is held by a live process (pid ${s.owner.pid} on ${s.owner.host}) — terminate that process first.`,
   137	      );
   138	    case 'cross-host':
   139	      throw new Error(
   140	        `lock is held from another host ('${s.owner.host}', this is '${io.host}') — cross-host locks are never provably safe to steal. ${ESCAPE_HATCH}`,
   141	      );
   142	    case 'torn':
   143	      // During ACQUIRE, torn metadata is usually a competitor caught between
   144	      // its mkdir and owner.json publication — transient by construction, so
   145	      // LockHeldError lets withLock wait it out (gate-2 fix 12). A genuinely
   146	      // crashed publisher exhausts the retry budget and surfaces this same
   147	      // message; recovery semantics (recoverLock) are unchanged: never steal.
   148	      throw new LockHeldError(`${s.cause} — unsupported for automatic recovery. ${ESCAPE_HATCH}`);
   149	    case 'dead': {
   150	      const token = publishOwner(io, p);
   151	      journalNote(io, p, `lock takeover: took over stale lock from dead pid ${s.owner.pid} (start time ${s.owner.startTime})`);
   152	      return token;
   153	    }
   154	    /* c8 ignore next 2 */
   155	    default:
   156	      throw new Error('unreachable lock state');
   157	  }
   158	}
   159	
   160	/**
   161	 * Fence-guarded release: the lock dir is removed only while owner.json still
   162	 * carries OUR token. A holder that paused past a takeover must not delete the
   163	 * competitor's lock on its way out — release is a write like any other.
   164	 * @param {any} io @param {ReturnType<typeof lockPaths>} p @param {string} token
   165	 */
   166	function release(io, p, token) {
   167	  try {
   168	    const current = JSON.parse(io.fs.readFileSync(p.owner, 'utf8'));
   169	    if (current.fencingToken !== token) return; // a competitor took over — their lock, not ours
   170	  } catch {
   171	    return; // owner metadata gone or torn mid-hold — never guess; leave it for recovery
   172	  }
   173	  io.fs.rmSync(p.lockDir, { recursive: true, force: true });
   174	}
   175	
   176	// A live same-host holder is transient by design — operation-scoped locks are
   177	// held for milliseconds — so contention waits briefly instead of dropping the
   178	// operation (gate-2 fix 12: simultaneous checkpoints must all land). Every
   179	// other contended state (torn, cross-host) stays fail-fast: waiting cannot
   180	// make those provably safe.
   181	const DEFAULT_RETRY = { attempts: 120, delayMs: 25 };
   182	
   183	/** Synchronous sleep without burning a core: Atomics.wait on a throwaway buffer. */
   184	const sleepMs = (/** @type {number} */ ms) => {
   185	  Atomics.wait(new Int32Array(new SharedArrayBuffer(4)), 0, 0, ms);
   186	};
   187	
   188	/**
   189	 * Acquire → run fn(token) → release (also on throw). Synchronous by design:
   190	 * everything the lock guards is sync fs work. Live-held locks are retried on
   191	 * a bounded budget (override via io.lockRetry = {attempts, delayMs}).
   192	 * @param {string} root @param {any} io @param {(token: string) => any} fn
   193	 */
   194	export function withLock(root, io, fn) {
   195	  const p = lockPaths(root);
   196	  const retry = io.lockRetry ?? DEFAULT_RETRY;
   197	  /** @type {string} */
   198	  let token;
   199	  for (let attempt = 0; ; attempt++) {
   200	    try {
   201	      token = acquire(io, p);
   202	      break;
   203	    } catch (err) {
   204	      if (err instanceof LockHeldError && attempt < retry.attempts) {
   205	        sleepMs(retry.delayMs);
   206	        continue;
   207	      }
   208	      throw err;
   209	    }
   210	  }
   211	  try {
   212	    return fn(token);
   213	  } finally {
   214	    release(io, p, token);
   215	  }
   216	}
   217	
   218	/**
   219	 * Fast-abort layer: re-read the published token immediately before a write; a
   220	 * holder that paused past its last check aborts instead of clobbering.
   221	 * @param {string} root @param {any} io @param {string} token @param {() => any} fn
   222	 */
   223	export function guardedWrite(root, io, token, fn) {
   224	  const p = lockPaths(root);
   225	  let current = null;
   226	  try {
   227	    current = JSON.parse(io.fs.readFileSync(p.owner, 'utf8'));
   228	  } catch {
   229	    throw new FencingError('lock owner metadata is gone — fencing check failed; aborting write');
   230	  }
   231	  if (current.fencingToken !== token) {
   232	    throw new FencingError(
   233	      `fencing token changed (held '${token}', current '${current.fencingToken}') — another process took over; aborting write`,
   234	    );
   235	  }
   236	  return fn();
   237	}
   238	
   239	/**
   240	 * Explicit recovery. `force` is an intent flag only — it never changes the
   241	 * decision: exactly one state recovers (provably-dead same-host owner).
   242	 * @param {string} root @param {any} io @param {{force?: boolean}} [opts]
   243	 * @returns {{recovered: boolean, refusedReason: string | null}}
   244	 */
   245	export function recoverLock(root, io, opts = {}) {
   246	  void opts;
   247	  const p = lockPaths(root);
   248	  const s = inspect(io, p);
   249	  switch (s.state) {
   250	    case 'free':
   251	      return { recovered: true, refusedReason: null };
   252	    case 'dead':
   253	      // Provably-dead owner: removal is justified by the verified state itself,
   254	      // not by holding the fence (there is no live holder to fence against).
   255	      io.fs.rmSync(p.lockDir, { recursive: true, force: true });
   256	      journalNote(io, p, `lock recovery: cleared stale lock of dead pid ${s.owner.pid}`);
   257	      return { recovered: true, refusedReason: null };
   258	    case 'live':
   259	      return {
   260	        recovered: false,
   261	        refusedReason: `lock is held by a live process (pid ${s.owner.pid} on ${s.owner.host}) — terminate that process first.`,
   262	      };
   263	    case 'cross-host':
   264	      return {
   265	        recovered: false,
   266	        refusedReason: `lock is held from another host ('${s.owner.host}', this is '${io.host}') — cross-host locks are never provably safe to steal, even with force. ${ESCAPE_HATCH}`,
   267	      };
   268	    case 'torn':
   269	      return {
   270	        recovered: false,
   271	        refusedReason: `${s.cause} — unsupported for automatic recovery, even with force. ${ESCAPE_HATCH}`,
   272	      };
   273	    /* c8 ignore next 2 */
   274	    default:
   275	      return { recovered: false, refusedReason: 'unreachable lock state' };
   276	  }
   277	}
     1	/**
     2	 * The --json contract: exactly one compact envelope line on stdout, nothing else.
     3	 * @param {any} io
     4	 * @param {{ok: boolean, data?: any, warnings?: any[], error?: {code: string, msg: string} | null}} env
     5	 */
     6	export function emitEnvelope(io, env) {
     7	  const full = { ok: env.ok, data: env.data ?? null, warnings: env.warnings ?? [], error: env.error ?? null };
     8	  io.stdout.write(JSON.stringify(full) + '\n');
     9	}
    10	
    11	/**
    12	 * Centralized usage-error emission (gate-2 fix 11): with --json the envelope
    13	 * contract holds even for bad invocations; otherwise a one-line stderr
    14	 * message. Always returns the usage exit code 2.
    15	 * @param {any} io @param {Record<string, string | boolean>} flags
    16	 * @param {string} cmd @param {string} msg
    17	 * @returns {number}
    18	 */
    19	export function usageError(io, flags, cmd, msg) {
    20	  if (flags.json === true) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: false, error: { code: 'usage', msg } });
    21	  else io.stderr.write(`baton ${cmd}: ${msg}\n`);
    22	  return 2;
    23	}
    24	
    25	/**
    26	 * Repo-root discovery (plan §Root discovery): `--root` wins; else the nearest
    27	 * ancestor of io.cwd carrying `.handoff/` or `baton.config.json`; else the
    28	 * nearest ancestor carrying `.git` (the repo toplevel — and a boundary the
    29	 * walk never crosses, so a nested repo resolves to itself); else io.cwd.
    30	 * @param {any} io @param {Record<string, string | boolean>} flags
    31	 * @returns {string}
    32	 */
    33	export function resolveRoot(io, flags) {
    34	  if (typeof flags.root === 'string' && flags.root.length > 0) return flags.root;
    35	  let dir = String(io.cwd);
    36	  while (true) {
    37	    try {
    38	      if (io.fs.existsSync(`${dir}/.handoff`) || io.fs.existsSync(`${dir}/baton.config.json`)) return dir;
    39	      if (io.fs.existsSync(`${dir}/.git`)) return dir; // repo toplevel; never walk past a .git boundary
    40	    } catch {
    41	      break;
    42	    }
    43	    const parent = dir.slice(0, dir.lastIndexOf('/')) || '/';
    44	    if (parent === dir) break;
    45	    dir = parent;
    46	  }
    47	  return String(io.cwd);
    48	}
    49	
    50	/**
    51	 * Minimal flag parser: `--key value` pairs (value = next token not starting
    52	 * with --), bare `--key` booleans, positionals collected in order.
    53	 * @param {string[]} args
    54	 * @returns {{flags: Record<string, string | boolean>, positionals: string[]}}
    55	 */
    56	export function parseFlags(args) {
    57	  /** @type {Record<string, string | boolean>} */
    58	  const flags = {};
    59	  /** @type {string[]} */
    60	  const positionals = [];
    61	  for (let i = 0; i < args.length; i += 1) {
    62	    const a = args[i];
    63	    if (a.startsWith('--')) {
    64	      const key = a.slice(2);
    65	      const next = args[i + 1];
    66	      if (next !== undefined && !next.startsWith('--')) {
    67	        flags[key] = next;
    68	        i += 1;
    69	      } else {
    70	        flags[key] = true;
    71	      }
    72	    } else {
    73	      positionals.push(a);
    74	    }
    75	  }
    76	  return { flags, positionals };
    77	}

    33	function version() {
    34	  const pkgPath = join(dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)), '..', '..', 'package.json');
    35	  return JSON.parse(readFileSync(pkgPath, 'utf8')).version;
    36	}
